<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove a conjecture of Erdős: for every sufficiently large integer <i>b</i>, every rational number $`a/b`$ with $`1\le a\lt b`$ is a sum of $`O(\log\log b)`$ distinct positive unit fractions, with an absolute implied constant. This order is best possible when the numerator varies. We also show that both the number of expansions of 1 with exactly <i>k</i> distinct terms and the least integer at least 2 that never occurs as a denominator in such an expansion grow doubly exponentially in <i>k</i>: their double logarithms have order <i>k</i>.

- **claim-uniform-length-order** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are absolute constants $c_1,c_2>0$ and $b_0$ such that, for every integer $b\ge b_0$, $$c_1\log\log b\le N(b)\le c_2\log\log b.$$ In particular, the upper bound holds for every integer numerator $1\le a<b$.
  - Explanation: The largest minimal Egyptian-fraction length over all numerators with a given denominator has double-logarithmic order, with absolute constants and no restriction on denominator size.
- **claim-counting-double-log** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-counting)): There are absolute constants $c,C>0$ and $k_0$ such that, for every integer $k\ge k_0$, $$ck\le\log\log F(k)\le Ck.$$
  - Explanation: The number of distinct k-term unit-fraction representations of one has double logarithm of linear order in k.
- **claim-prescribed-denominator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-prescribed)): For every sufficiently large integer $k$, $$\exp\bigl(\exp(k/600)\bigr)\le v(k)\le1+k^{2^{k-1}}.$$ More precisely, $$\frac{\log2}{257} \le\liminf_{k\to\infty}\frac{\log\log v(k)}k \le\limsup_{k\to\infty}\frac{\log\log v(k)}k \le\log2.$$ Equivalently for the lower bound, every fixed $c<\log2/257$ satisfies $v(k)\ge\exp(\exp(ck))$ for every sufficiently large integer $k$. In particular $\log\log v(k)=\Theta(k)$.
  - Explanation: The least integer that is never an exact denominator of a k-term expansion of one is doubly exponential in k, with explicit lower and upper bounds and liminf/limsup slopes.
- **claim-dense-set** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-density)): There are absolute constants $D_M>1$, $L>0$, and $S_0>1$ with the following property. Put $D_X=D_M+2$ and $D_C=4D_X$. For every real $S\ge S_0$ and every integer $C$ satisfying $$e^{D_CS}\le C\le e^{2D_CS},$$ there are an integer $M$ and a set $G\subseteq\{1,\ldots,\lfloor X\rfloor\}$, where $X=e^{D_XS}$, such that $$e^S<M\le e^{D_MS}, \qquad \bigl\|\{1,\ldots,\lfloor X\rfloor\}\setminus G\bigr\|\le \frac X8.$$ For each $u\in G$, the fraction $u/(MC)$ is a sum of at most $L\log S$ unit fractions with integer denominators at least $2$. Repetitions are allowed.
  - Explanation: For a denominator factor in a fixed exponential range there is an auxiliary integer M such that all but a small proportion of numerators u give short expansions of u/(MC).
- **claim-remove-repetitions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-distinct)): Let $k\ge1$ be an integer. If $0<x<1$ is a sum of $k$ positive unit fractions with integer denominators, then it is a sum of $k$ such fractions with distinct denominators, all at least $2$.
  - Explanation: A sum below one of a fixed number of unit fractions can be rewritten with distinct denominators without increasing the number of terms.
- **claim-greedy-preparation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-greedy)): Let $1\le a<b$ be integers and let $T>b$ be real. After at most $$1+\left\lceil\log_2\left(\frac{\log T}{\log 2}\right)\right\rceil$$ greedy steps, either $a/b$ has been expressed as a sum of unit fractions, or its positive remainder can be written as $A/C$, with integers $$1\le A\le a,\qquad T\le C<T^2.$$ All unit denominators produced are at least $2$. The fractions used during this procedure need not be reduced.
  - Explanation: A short greedy phase either finishes the expansion or leaves a remainder with numerator controlled and denominator in a prescribed range.
- **claim-divisor-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-divisor)): For every fixed pair of real numbers $D,r\ge1$, there is $S_0(D,r)$ such that the following holds for $S\ge S_0(D,r)$. Suppose that $X,Y$ are real numbers and $N$ is a nonnegative integer satisfying $$\frac{S}{2\log S}\le \log X\le DS,\qquad X^{1/2}\le Y\le X,\qquad N\le e^{DS}.$$ Then $$\label{eq:divisor-moment} \sum_{1\le h\le Y}d_X(N+h)^r\le Y\exp(S^{1/4}).$$
  - Explanation: The r-th moment of the truncated divisor count over a shifted interval is nearly linear in the interval length, uniformly in the shift.
- **claim-small-residue-divisors** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-residues)): For all sufficiently large $S$ and every integer $C$ satisfying <a href="#eq:C-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:C-range">[eq:C-range]</a>, there are an integer $M$ and indexed lists $T_0,T_1,\ldots,T_R$, each with $2^m$ entries, such that:* 1. *$e^S<M\le e^{D_MS}$, and the least power of $2$ at least $S^{D_0}$ divides $M$. Every entry of every $T_i$ divides $M$.* 2. *For each $0\le j<d$, let $T=T_0$ if $X_j>e^S$, and let $T=T_1$ otherwise. Apart from at most $X_j e^{-c_*m}$ integers $u\in(X_{j+1},X_j]$, at least $\rho \|T\|/2$ entries $t\in T$ satisfy $$h_{j,t}(u)\le X_{j+1}, \qquad c_*=0.001.$$* 3. *For every integer $S^{D_0}<u\le e^m$, some entry $t$ of one of $T_1,\ldots,T_R$ satisfies $$u\lceil t/u\rceil-t\le u^{1-\eta}.$$* *Repeated values in a list are counted with their indices. The threshold on $S$ is independent of $C$.
  - Explanation: An auxiliary integer and lists of its divisors are built so that most numerators at each level, and every terminal numerator, admit a divisor giving a small residue.
- **claim-discrepancy-residue** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-discrepancy)): For all sufficiently large real $w$, suppose $u,Q$ are positive integers and $T$ is a nonempty finite list of positive integers. If $$\left\|\frac1{\|T\|}\sum_{t\in T}\mathrm e(\ell Qt/u)\right\| \le e^{-3\eta w} \quad \left(1\le\ell\le\lfloor e^{4\eta w}\rfloor\right),$$ then at least $e^{-\eta w}\|T\|/2$ entries satisfy $$0\le u\lceil Qt/u\rceil-Qt<e^{-\eta w}u.$$
  - Explanation: Strong cancellation of exponential sums over a list of integers forces a positive proportion of entries to give small least residues.
- **claim-reciprocal-phase-sum** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-reciprocal-phase)): Fix $B\ge4$. There are constants $A_B,\delta_B>0$ such that, for every sufficiently large $U$, every real $Z$ with $U^4\le \|Z\|\le U^B$, and every interval $I\subset[U,2U]$, $$\left\|\sum_{n\in I}\mathrm e(Z/n)\right\|\le A_BU^{1-\delta_B}.$$
  - Explanation: Exponential sums of the reciprocal phase over a dyadic interval have a power saving, obtained by van der Corput differencing.
- **claim-deterministic-mean** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-deterministic)): For the deterministic list $T_0$ constructed above, every level $0\le j<d$ with $X=X_j>e^S$, and every integer $1\le\ell\le H:=\lfloor e^{4\eta m}\rfloor$, one has $$\label{eq:deterministic-mean} \frac1X\sum_{X_{j+1}<u\le X} \left\|\frac1{\|T_0\|}\sum_{t\in T_0}\mathrm e(\ell Ct/u)\right\|^2 \le e^{-0.01m}$$ for all sufficiently large $S$, uniformly in $C$.
  - Explanation: For the deterministic list of prime subset products the mean square of the normalized exponential sums is exponentially small at high levels.
- **claim-random-second-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-random)): Let $K=100$, $D_0=100000$, and $m=\lfloor S/\log S\rfloor$. Let $\mathcal P$ be a set of primes in $[S^K,2S^K]$ with $P=\|\mathcal P\|\ge S^{K-1}$, and sample $$p_{j,\epsilon}\qquad (1\le j\le m,\ \epsilon\in\{0,1\})$$ independently and uniformly from $\mathcal P$. For $I\in\{0,1\}^m$, put $t_I=\prod_{j=1}^m p_{j,I_j}$. For every integer $u$ such that $$D_0\log S\le V:=\log u\le S, \qquad w=\min(m,V),$$ and every integer $1\le l\le \exp(0.005w)$, one has $$\label{eq:random-second-moment} \mathbb E\left\|2^{-m}\sum_{I\in\{0,1\}^m}\mathrm e(lt_I/u)\right\|^2 \le \exp(-0.01w)$$ for all sufficiently large $S$. The onset is absolute and uniform in $u,l,\mathcal P$.
  - Explanation: Random products of sampled primes give an exponentially small second moment of the normalized exponential sum modulo u.
- **claim-marked-cleanup** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-cleanup)): Let $Q>1$ be odd. Suppose that a finite list of positive integers $m_1,\ldots,m_t$ satisfies $\sum_{i=1}^t1/m_i=1$ and contains a multiple of $Q$. Then $1$ has a representation by at most $t$ distinct positive unit fractions, still with a denominator divisible by $Q$.
  - Explanation: Repetitions in an expansion of one can be removed without lengthening it while keeping a denominator divisible by a given odd integer.
- **claim-greedy-prefix-avoiding-marker** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-greedy-prefix)): Let $m\ge4$ and $T\ge2m^2$ be integers. There are integers $2\le n_1<\cdots<n_j$, none equal to $m$, and integers $q>0$ and $0\le R<2m$ such that $$1=\frac1m+\sum_{i=1}^{j}\frac1{n_i}+\frac Rq, \qquad q=m\prod_{i=1}^{j}n_i, \qquad j<3+\log_2\log_2 T.$$ Writing $q_0=m$ and $q_i=m\prod_{h=1}^{i}n_h$ for $1\le i\le j$, we have $n_i\le q_{i-1}+1$ for every $1\le i\le j$. If $R>0$, then $$q\ge T,\qquad \frac Rq<\frac{2m}{T}\le\frac1m, \qquad \frac Rq<\frac1{n_i}\quad(1\le i\le j).$$ In either case $q<T^2$.
  - Explanation: A greedy prefix that skips one reserved denominator leaves an unreduced remainder smaller than every reserved reciprocal.
- **claim-marker-padding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exact-marker-padding)): Let $r\ge3$ be an integer, and suppose that $$1=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\frac1{n_i},\qquad 1\le n_1<\cdots<n_r,$$ where the denominators are integers. For every $m\in\{n_1,\ldots,n_r\}$ there is a representation of $1$ by exactly $r+1$ distinct positive unit fractions that still contains the exact denominator $m$. Consequently $D_r\subseteq D_{r+1}$.
  - Explanation: A distinct expansion of one can be lengthened by one term while retaining any chosen denominator, so the sets of attainable denominators increase with length.
- **claim-marked-length** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-marked-length)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is an integer $m_\varepsilon$ such that every integer $m\ge m_\varepsilon$ occurs as an exact denominator in a representation of $1$ by distinct positive unit fractions with at most $$\left(\frac{257}{\log 2}+\varepsilon\right)\log\log m$$ terms.
  - Explanation: Every large integer m is an exact denominator of a distinct expansion of one whose length is an explicit constant multiple of the double logarithm of m.
- **claim-rational-divisor-supply** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-divisor-supply)): For every sufficiently large integer $m$, put $$L=\log\log m,\qquad Y=m^4,\qquad \ell(v)=\left\lceil\frac{4\log v}{\log 2}\right\rceil \quad(v\geq2).$$ Let $P(v)$ be the product of the first $\ell(v)$ primes, and define $$K_m=P(Y)^2\,\lfloor L\rfloor!.$$ Then $K_m>m$ and $$\label{eq:supply-size} \log K_m\leq \left(\frac{32}{\log 2}+o(1)\right)\log m\log\log m \qquad(m\longrightarrow\infty).$$ Moreover, every integer $1\leq s\leq Y$ has a representation $$\label{eq:rational-supply} s=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\frac{e_i}{t_i},\qquad h\leq16,\quad e_i\mid K_m,\quad e_i,t_i\in\mathbb Z_{>0}.$$ Repeated summands are allowed.
  - Explanation: A moderately sized integer is built whose divisors, divided by integers, represent every small positive integer with boundedly many summands.
- **claim-marked-divisor-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-divisor-density)): Let $m\ge2$ be an integer. Suppose that $q$ is obtained from $m$ by a finite sequence of replacements $q\mapsto nq$, where at each replacement $n$ is a positive integer satisfying $n\le q+1$. Then, for every real number $w$ with $1\le w\le q$, there is a positive divisor $d$ of $q$ such that $$\frac wm\le d\le w.$$
  - Explanation: A denominator built by step-bounded multiplications has a divisor in every multiplicative window of the stated width.
- **claim-marked-grouping** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-grouping)): Let $m\ge2$, $q\ge1$, $K_m\ge1$, and $B\ge1$ be integers. Suppose that, for every real $w\in[1,q]$, the integer $q$ has a divisor in $[w/m,w]$. Suppose also that each integer $s\in[1,m^4]$ can be written as a sum of at most $B$ positive rational numbers $e/t$, where $e\mid K_m$ and $t$ is a positive integer. Then, for every integer $X$ with $1\le X\le q$, the rational number $X/(qK_m)$ is a sum of at most $$B\left(\frac{\log X}{2\log m}+2\right)$$ positive unit fractions, with repetitions allowed.
  - Explanation: A numerator is split into few groups using dense divisors, and each group is expanded by the rational supply, bounding the unit-fraction count.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Egyptian-fraction expansion** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An Egyptian-fraction expansion of a positive rational number is a finite sum of distinct reciprocals of positive integers.
- **N(a,b) and N(b)** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For integers $1\le a<b$, let $N(a,b)$ be the least $k$ for which $$\frac ab=\frac1{n_1}+\cdots+\frac1{n_k}, \qquad 2\le n_1<\cdots<n_k,\qquad n_i\in\mathbb Z,$$ and put $N(b)=\max_{1\le a<b}N(a,b)$.
- **F(k)** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For each positive integer $k$, put $$F(k)=\#\left\{(n_1,\ldots,n_k): 1\le n_1<\cdots<n_k,\quad n_i\in\mathbb Z,\quad \frac1{n_1}+\cdots+\frac1{n_k}=1\right\}.$$
- **D_k** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For each integer $k\ge1$, let $D_k$ be the set of integers $m\ge2$ for which there are positive integers $n_1<\cdots<n_k$ satisfying $$1=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac1{n_i},\qquad m\in\{n_1,\ldots,n_k\}.$$
- **v(k)** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Set $$v(k)=\min\bigl(\{2,3,\ldots\}\setminus D_k\bigr).$$
- **Truncated divisor count d_X(n)** ([source](#sec:divisors)): for $X\ge1$ and $n\in\mathbb N$, put $$d_X(n)=\#\{a\in\mathbb N:a\le X,\ a\mid n\}.$$
- **Least residue h_{j,t}(u)** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For $0\le j\le d$, write $$\label{eq:least-residue} h_{j,t}(u)=u\left\lceil\frac{C_jt}{u}\right\rceil-C_jt.$$ In particular, $h_{j,t}(u)$ is the least nonnegative residue of $-C_jt$ modulo $u$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-remove-repetitions** [claim](#stmt-lem-distinct) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-distinct): Finiteness of candidate lists plus splitting identities that raise the denominator sum. -> distinct denominators, same length
- **step-greedy** [claim](#stmt-lem-greedy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-greedy): Greedy subtraction with squaring decay of the remainder. -> remainder A/C with C in the prescribed range
- **step-divisor-moment** [claim](#stmt-lem-divisor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-divisor): Prime-factor prefix splitting, Rankin weighting and a band decomposition of the next prime. -> uniform moment bound
- **step-discrepancy** [claim](#stmt-lem-discrepancy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-discrepancy): Erdős–Turán inequality applied to the fractional parts of -Qt/u. -> positive proportion of small residues
- **step-reciprocal-phase** [claim](#stmt-lem-reciprocal-phase) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reciprocal-phase): Repeated van der Corput differencing then a second-derivative estimate. -> power-saving bound for the reciprocal phase sum
- **step-deterministic-mean** [claim](#stmt-lem-deterministic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-deterministic): Expand the square, split off the diagonal, bound off-diagonal pairs by the reciprocal phase lemma. -> exponentially small mean square
- **step-random-moment** [claim](#stmt-lem-random) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-random): Hamming-distance concentration, exposure of fresh primes and additive-character orthogonality. -> second-moment bound
- **step-residue-lists** [claim](#stmt-lem-residues) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-random-2): Markov inequalities and union bounds over levels and terminal integers to fix one realization. -> integer M with residue properties
- **step-density** [claim](#stmt-prop-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-random-3): Backward construction of good sets, predecessor double count, Hölder inequality and recurrence unrolling. -> dense set of numerators with short expansions
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-greedy-2): Greedy prefix, sum of two dense-set numerators, scaling, and a direct denominator-growth argument for the lower bound. -> uniform order of N(b)
- **step-marked-cleanup** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-cleanup) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-cleanup): Parity-dependent splitting of repeated denominators and a lexicographic termination argument. -> distinct expansion keeping a multiple of Q
- **step-counting** [claim](#stmt-cor-counting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-cleanup-2): Short expansion with divisor-rich denominator, branching over divisors, injective padding, and a product recurrence for the upper bound. -> double-log order of F(k)
- **step-greedy-prefix** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-greedy-prefix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-greedy-prefix): Greedy iteration with a single exceptional step avoiding the marker, unreduced fractions. -> prefix and small remainder
- **step-marker-padding** [claim](#stmt-lem-exact-marker-padding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exact-marker-padding): Splitting identities with a case analysis on the two largest denominators and a congruence argument for a prime. -> one longer expansion containing m
- **step-rational-supply** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-divisor-supply) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-divisor-supply): Larger-sieve pair counting, quantitative three-prime theorem and bilinear character sums. -> rational representations of small integers
- **step-divisor-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-divisor-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-divisor-density): Induction over the replacement steps. -> divisors in every multiplicative window
- **step-grouping** [claim](#stmt-lem-marked-grouping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-grouping): Repeated removal of a divisor-multiple group, then rational supply for each group. -> bound on unit fractions for X/(qK_m)
- **step-marked-length** [claim](#stmt-prop-marked-length) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-grouping-2): Combine prefix, grouping and the distinct-removal lemma on the tail. -> explicit length bound for a marked expansion
- **step-prescribed-denominator** [claim](#stmt-cor-prescribed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-marked-grouping-3): Iterate marker-preserving padding to every length and use the product recurrence for the upper bound. -> bounds on v(k)

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-prefix-identity**: The reserved reciprocal of m, the prefix unit fractions and the unreduced remainder fraction add up exactly to one, and the final denominator is m times the product of the prefix denominators. The prefix denominators are increasing, at least two, and never equal to m.
- **inv-prefix-bounds**: The remainder numerator is nonnegative and below twice m, each prefix denominator is at most the preceding partial denominator plus one, and the number of prefix terms is below the stated double-logarithmic bound in T.
- **inv-remainder-small**: When the remainder is positive, its denominator is at least T and below the square of T, and the remainder fraction is smaller than the reciprocal of m and than every prefix reciprocal.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-fixed-width-denominators**: Run the prefix construction in fixed-width machine integers. -> Denominators multiply at each step and quickly exceed machine registers, so the output silently wraps and the identity fails.
- **anti-cancel-common-factors**: Reduce the remainder fraction to lowest terms after each step to keep numbers small. -> Cancelling destroys the product formula for the denominator and the divisor structure that later steps rely on, so the stated bounds no longer apply.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. Lemma 14 gives an explicit finite procedure: start from the pair (m-1, m) and repeatedly apply a ceiling-based update, replacing the denominator m by m+1 when it would be chosen, until the remainder vanishes or the denominator reaches T.

```python
# INPUTS: two integers m and T on stdin separated by whitespace.
# PRECONDITIONS: m >= 4 and T >= 2*m*m.
# OUTPUTS: one line: R q n1 ... nj (remainder numerator, unreduced denominator, prefix denominators).
# TIME: number of loop steps is doubly logarithmic in T; each step is big-integer arithmetic.
# SPACE: O(j) integers of growing size.
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers, ceiling division by negated floor division.
# FAILURE: inputs violating the preconditions are outside the contract; the loop still terminates.
import sys

def main():
    m, T = map(int, sys.stdin.read().split())
    R, q = m - 1, m
    ns = []
    while R > 0 and q < T:
        a = -(-q // R)
        n = a if a != m else m + 1
        R, q = n * R - q, n * q
        ns.append(n)
    print(R, q, *ns)

main()
```

        "observed_exit": 1,
        "receipt_sha256": "b436f55142c205dd627ab70c6602d2ff260b095eb6d99c280161a53727fa261c"
      }
    ],
    "oracle_code": "import sys, math\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef main():\n    m, T = map(int, open(sys.argv[1]).read().split())\n    out = open(sys.argv[2]).read().split()\n    exp = sys.argv[3].split()\n    if out != exp:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    v = list(map(int, out))\n    R, q, ns = v[0], v[1], v[2:]\n    j = len(ns)\n    tot = Fraction(1, m) + sum(Fraction(1, n) for n in ns) + Fraction(R, q)\n    if tot != 1:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    prod = m\n    prev = m\n    last = 1\n    for n in ns:\n        if n < 2 or n == m or n <= last:\n            sys.exit(1)\n        if n > prev + 1:\n            sys.exit(1)\n        last = n\n        prev = prev * n\n    if prev != q:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    if not (0 <= R < 2 * m):\n        sys.exit(1)\n    if not (j < 3 + math.log2(math.log2(T))):\n        sys.exit(1)\n    if q >= T * T:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    if R > 0:\n        if q < T:\n            sys.exit(1)\n        if not (R * T < 2 * m * q and 2 * m * m <= T):\n            sys.exit(1)\n        for n in ns:\n            if not (R * n < q):\n                sys.exit(1)\n    sys.exit(0)\n\nmain()\n",
    "output_contract": "One line R q n1 ... nj such that 1 = 1/m + sum 1/n_i + R/q with the properties of Lemma 14.",
    "reason": "Lemma 14 gives an explicit finite procedure: start from the pair (m-1, m) and repeatedly apply a ceiling-based update, replacing the denominator m by m+1 when it would be chosen, until the remainder vanishes or the denominator reaches T.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: two integers m and T on stdin separated by whitespace.\n# PRECONDITIONS: m >= 4 and T >= 2*m*m.\n# OUTPUTS: one line: R q n1 ... nj (remainder numerator, unreduced denominator, prefix denominators).\n# TIME: number of loop steps is doubly logarithmic in T; each step is big-integer arithmetic.\n# SPACE: O(j) integers of growing size.\n# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers, ceiling division by negated floor division.\n# FAILURE: inputs violating the preconditions are outside the contract; the loop still terminates.\nimport sys\n\ndef main():\n    m, T = map(int, sys.stdin.read().split())\n    R, q = m - 1, m\n    ns = []\n    while R > 0 and q < T:\n        a = -(-q // R)\n        n = a if a != m else m + 1\n        R, q = n * R - q, n * q\n        ns.append(n)\n    print(R, q, *ns)\n\nmain()\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "2 40 2 5",
        "input": "4 32"
      },
      {
        "expected": "0 800 2 4 20",
        "input": "5 50"
      },

      },

        "input": "8 128"
      }
    ]
  },

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorem is an existence bound on minimal expansion lengths. The proof is probabilistic, and the paper states no explicit procedure whose output a library could compute. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No simulation, game or dynamical model appears in the paper. The objects are exact rational sums of unit fractions. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool or utility is specified. The greedy preparation step is only a proof device inside a non-constructive argument. |
| algorithms | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Some pieces are explicit finite procedures that could be adapted: the greedy prefix, repetition removal, and padding that preserves a marker. The full short-expansion bound relies on a random choice of the auxiliary integer M and gives no efficient algorithm. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper introduces no data structure. Indexed lists of divisors are proof objects, not stored structures with operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Denominators grow doubly exponentially and are unrestricted, so fixed-width hardware arithmetic cannot represent the expansions in general. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper makes no security, hardness or protocol claim. Prime products appear only as divisor lists inside the proof. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning, statistical estimation or model fitting is involved. Random sampling of primes is a proof device only. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The quantity N(b) is a worst-case minimal length. The paper offers no optimisation algorithm, and a constraint model would be unsupported. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No distributed, concurrent or networked setting appears in the paper. |
| other (formal mathematics and number-theory reference) | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The results give sharp orders for expansion length, for the count of expansions of one, and for the least omitted denominator. These are useful as reference bounds for formal libraries and for calibrating experiments, not as computational constructions. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-denominators-unrestricted** (hypothesis): The length bounds are proved with no restriction on denominator size. The paper optimises only the order of the length, so the double-logarithmic bound says nothing about expansions whose denominators are capped. Blocked use: ['Applying the double-logarithmic length bound to clock dividers or rate limiters with bounded denominators', 'Storing the expansions in fixed-width integer types']
- **limit-constants-not-explicit** (formal_scope): The main theorem asserts absolute constants and a threshold without giving their values. It cannot be used to certify a concrete length for a concrete denominator. Blocked use: ['Quoting numeric values for the constants in the main theorem', 'Using the upper bound to guarantee a specific expansion length for a given b']
- **limit-probabilistic-auxiliary-integer** (nonconstructive): The auxiliary integer M is obtained by showing that a good realization of random prime samples exists. No explicit choice or efficient search procedure is given. Blocked use: ['Implementing the short-expansion bound as an efficient algorithm', 'Claiming a deterministic construction of M']
- **limit-dense-set-not-all-numerators** (edge_case): The dense-set proposition gives short expansions only for numerators in a set G that omits a small proportion of numerators. Passing to every numerator needs the separate sum-of-two-members argument. Blocked use: ['Assuming every numerator u below X has a short expansion of u/(MC)', 'Reusing the proposition for a fixed, possibly exceptional numerator']
- **limit-greedy-stage-range** (hypothesis): The greedy preparation stage requires a numerator below the denominator and a threshold larger than the denominator. Outside this range its step count and remainder range are not asserted. Blocked use: ['Applying the greedy lemma to improper fractions', 'Using the greedy stage with a threshold at most b']
- **limit-repetition-removal-below-one** (hypothesis): The repetition-removal lemma needs a total strictly between zero and one. At total one a different argument is required, and it keeps only a multiple of a chosen odd integer. Blocked use: ['Applying the first lemma to expansions of one', 'Assuming an exact denominator survives the cleanup at total one']
- **limit-divisor-moment-order-fixed** (hypothesis): The divisor moment bound holds for a moment exponent fixed before the main parameter grows. The threshold depends on that exponent, so it cannot grow with S. Blocked use: ['Letting the moment order grow with S in the descent', 'Using the moment bound with a threshold chosen after the order']
- **limit-prefix-unreduced-pair** (edge_case): The marker-avoiding greedy prefix is stated for the unreduced pair of remainder numerator and denominator. Reducing it changes the denominator product and the divisor structure used later. Blocked use: ['Cancelling common factors of the remainder fraction in the greedy prefix', 'Feeding a reduced remainder into the divisor-grouping step']
- **limit-padding-three-terms** (hypothesis): Marker-preserving padding is stated for expansions of one with at least three terms. Shorter expansions are outside the lemma. Blocked use: ['Padding the one-term or two-term expansions of one', 'Assuming a marker can always be kept for any starting length']
- **limit-endpoint-slope-not-asserted** (formal_scope): The lower slope for the least omitted denominator holds for each constant below the stated endpoint. The paper explicitly does not assert the endpoint inequality or a sharp limiting slope. Blocked use: ['Claiming the endpoint constant itself in an eventual inequality', 'Stating that the limit of the double logarithm over k exists']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 025 (`lean/docs/025.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Short Egyptian fractions](../../preprints/Short-Egyptian-fractions-September-25-2026/Short-Egyptian-fractions-September-25-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> An Egyptian-fraction expansion writes a rational number as a sum of distinct unit fractions. The formalization proves that every $a/b$ with $1\le a<b$ has such an expansion and that the largest minimum length at denominator $b$ is $\Theta(\log\log b)$. If $F(k)$ counts exact $k$-term expansions of $1$, it also proves $\log\log F(k)=\Theta(k)$ and bounds every denominator in such an expansion.
>
> Every integer $m\ge2$ occurs as a denominator in an expansion of $1$, with length eventually at most $(257/\log2+\varepsilon)\log\log m$ for every $\varepsilon>0$; padding can preserve that denominator. If $v(k)$ is the least integer at least $2$ absent from all exact $k$-term expansions of $1$, then eventually $e^{e^{k/600}}\le v(k)\le1+k^{2^{k-1}}$, and $\liminf_{k\to\infty}\log\log v(k)/k\ge\log2/257$.

Comparator source `EgyptianFractions.lean` (3863 bytes, sha256 `e96f5967735c7f7af64fc306601a5280d20f5752799e8c9a5fcc424cd421c0f6`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open scoped BigOperators

namespace Problem337

def IsEgyptianExpansion {k : ℕ} (x : ℚ) (n : Fin k → ℕ) : Prop :=
  (∀ i, 2 ≤ n i) ∧ StrictMono n ∧
    (∑ i : Fin k, (1 : ℚ) / (n i : ℚ)) = x

def egyptianLengths (a b : ℕ) : Set ℕ :=
  {k | ∃ n : Fin k → ℕ,
    IsEgyptianExpansion ((a : ℚ) / (b : ℚ)) n}

def egyptianLength (a b : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  sInf (egyptianLengths a b)

def maxEgyptianLength (b : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  (Finset.range b).sup (fun a => if 1 ≤ a then egyptianLength a b else 0)

def IsOneExpansion {k : ℕ} (n : Fin k → ℕ) : Prop :=
  (∀ i, 1 ≤ n i) ∧ StrictMono n ∧
    (∑ i : Fin k, (1 : ℚ) / (n i : ℚ)) = 1

def OneExpansions (k : ℕ) : Set (Fin k → ℕ) :=
  {n | IsOneExpansion n}

def F (k : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  (OneExpansions k).ncard

def D (k : ℕ) : Set ℕ :=
  {m | 2 ≤ m ∧ ∃ n : Fin k → ℕ,
    IsOneExpansion n ∧ ∃ i : Fin k, n i = m}

def missingDenominators (k : ℕ) : Set ℕ :=
  {m : ℕ | 2 ≤ m} \ D k

def v (k : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  sInf (missingDenominators k)

def prescribedSlope (k : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  Real.log (Real.log (v k : ℝ)) / (k : ℝ)

theorem egyptian_length_is_minimum :
    ∀ a b : ℕ, 1 ≤ a → a < b →
  (∃ n : Fin (egyptianLength a b) → ℕ,
    IsEgyptianExpansion ((a : ℚ) / (b : ℚ)) n) ∧
  (∀ k : ℕ, (∃ n : Fin k → ℕ,
    IsEgyptianExpansion ((a : ℚ) / (b : ℚ)) n) →
    egyptianLength a b ≤ k) := by
  sorry

theorem main_double_log_order :
    ∃ c1 c2 : ℝ, 0 < c1 ∧ 0 < c2 ∧ ∃ b0 : ℕ,
  ∀ b : ℕ, b0 ≤ b →
    c1 * Real.log (Real.log (b : ℝ)) ≤ (maxEgyptianLength b : ℝ) ∧
    (maxEgyptianLength b : ℝ) ≤ c2 * Real.log (Real.log (b : ℝ)) := by
  sorry

theorem one_expansions_finite_and_bounded :
    (∀ k : ℕ, Set.Finite (OneExpansions k) ∧
    F k ≤ k ^ (2 ^ k - 1)) ∧
  (∀ k : ℕ, ∀ n : Fin k → ℕ, IsOneExpansion n →
    ∀ i : Fin k, n i ≤ k ^ (2 ^ i.val)) := by
  sorry

theorem counting_double_log_order :
    ∃ c C : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ 0 < C ∧ ∃ k0 : ℕ,
  ∀ k : ℕ, k0 ≤ k →
    c * (k : ℝ) ≤ Real.log (Real.log (F k : ℝ)) ∧
    Real.log (Real.log (F k : ℝ)) ≤ C * (k : ℝ) := by
  sorry

theorem exact_marker_padding :
    ∀ r : ℕ, 3 ≤ r → ∀ n : Fin r → ℕ, IsOneExpansion n →
  ∀ m : ℕ, (∃ i : Fin r, n i = m) →
    ∃ n' : Fin (r + 1) → ℕ,
      IsOneExpansion n' ∧ ∃ i : Fin (r + 1), n' i = m := by
  sorry

theorem every_exact_marker_occurs :
    ∀ m : ℕ, 2 ≤ m → ∃ k : ℕ, m ∈ D k := by
  sorry

theorem quantitative_marked_length :
    ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ mε : ℕ, 2 ≤ mε ∧
  ∀ m : ℕ, mε ≤ m →
    ∃ k : ℕ, ∃ n : Fin k → ℕ,
      IsOneExpansion n ∧
      (∃ i : Fin k, n i = m) ∧
      (k : ℝ) ≤ (257 / Real.log 2 + ε) * Real.log (Real.log (m : ℝ)) := by
  sorry

theorem missing_denominator_semantics :
    ∀ k : ℕ,
  Set.Finite (D k) ∧
  (∃ m : ℕ, m ∈ missingDenominators k) ∧
  2 ≤ v k ∧ v k ∉ D k ∧
  (∀ m : ℕ, 2 ≤ m → m < v k → m ∈ D k) ∧
  (1 ≤ k → v k ≤ 1 + k ^ (2 ^ (k - 1))) := by
  sorry

theorem prescribed_denominator_corollary :
    (∃ k0 : ℕ, ∀ k : ℕ, k0 ≤ k →
    Real.exp (Real.exp ((k : ℝ) / 600)) ≤ (v k : ℝ) ∧
    v k ≤ 1 + k ^ (2 ^ (k - 1))) ∧
  (Real.log 2 / 257 ≤ Filter.liminf prescribedSlope Filter.atTop ∧
    Filter.liminf prescribedSlope Filter.atTop ≤
      Filter.limsup prescribedSlope Filter.atTop ∧
    Filter.limsup prescribedSlope Filter.atTop ≤ Real.log 2) ∧
  (∀ c : ℝ, c < Real.log 2 / 257 → ∃ k0 : ℕ,
    ∀ k : ℕ, k0 ≤ k →
      Real.exp (Real.exp (c * (k : ℝ))) ≤ (v k : ℝ)) := by
  sorry

end Problem337

end

end OAI
```

    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Short Egyptian fractions](../../preprints/Short-Egyptian-fractions-September-25-2026/Short-Egyptian-fractions-September-25-2026.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nAn Egyptian-fraction expansion writes a rational number as a sum of distinct unit fractions. The formalization proves that every $a/b$ with $1\\le a<b$ has such an expansion and that the largest minimum length at denominator $b$ is $\\Theta(\\log\\log b)$. If $F(k)$ counts exact $k$-term expansions of $1$, it also proves $\\log\\log F(k)=\\Theta(k)$ and bounds every denominator in such an expansion.\n\nEvery integer $m\\ge2$ occurs as a denominator in an expansion of $1$, with length eventually at most $(257/\\log2+\\varepsilon)\\log\\log m$ for every $\\varepsilon>0$; padding can preserve that denominator. If $v(k)$ is the least integer at least $2$ absent from all exact $k$-term expansions of $1$, then eventually $e^{e^{k/600}}\\le v(k)\\le1+k^{2^{k-1}}$, and $\\liminf_{k\\to\\infty}\\log\\log v(k)/k\\ge\\log2/257$."
  },
  "status": "present",
  "toolchain": "leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1"
}

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove a conjecture of Erdős: for every sufficiently large integer <i>b</i>, every rational number $`a/b`$ with $`1\le a\lt b`$ is a sum of $`O(\log\log b)`$ distinct positive unit fractions, with an absolute implied constant. This order is best possible when the numerator varies. We also show that both the number of expansions of 1 with exactly <i>k</i> distinct terms and the least integer at least 2 that never occurs as a denominator in such an expansion grow doubly exponentially in <i>k</i>: their double logarithms have order <i>k</i>.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
